Mirror descent algorithm details:
Input a (sum n), β (lambda/n).
Function compute_loss_grad(a, λ):
- c=conv(a,a)
- m=max(c); z=exp((λ/n? wait β) * (c-m)); W=z.sum(); p=z/W.
- smooth_loss_exact_scale = (2.0/n)*(1.0/β)* (m + log(W)/? Actually logZ = m*β? We can compute logZ = m + log(W) if β=1? Let's define t=β. logsumexp(t c)= t*m + log(sum exp(t(c-m))). smooth=(2/n)*(logsumexp)/t = (2/n)*(m + log(W)/t). If t=λ/n, smooth=(2/n)*(m + (n/λ) log W). For large λ, approx max.
But we may just use exact max for tracking; for gradient use p.
- cross = conv(p, a_rev) sliced. Need p length L. Use FFT.
- diag = p[0::2] * a, where p indices 0,2,...,2n-2. length n.
- d = 2*cross - diag.
- grad_a = (2.0/n)*d.
- mu = dot(a, grad_a)/n.
- grad_theta = a*(grad_a - mu). (Check natural gradient formula: a_j(grad_a_j - μ) with μ = (1/n)∑ a_i grad_a_i? Earlier μ = dot(a,l)/n. Yes.)
Return smooth, cmax, grad_theta, maybe p.

Let's think about active set Gauss-Newton in more detail; might be a game changer.

We want minimize F= max c_k (since scale fixed). At a local optimum, the set of active peaks A where c_k=M. For small perturbation δ with sum 0, we need for all active k: linear derivative ≤0 (to not increase max), and probably complementary slackness. This is similar to nonlinear airborne. We can enforce all active c_k reduced. Gauss-Newton fitting c_k to target T<M for k in A can reduce them.

Suppose we choose target T = M - Δ (a small reduction). Solve min ||W(Aδ + c_A - T)||² + μ||δ||², sum δ=0, δ_i ≥ -a_i/step. For small Δ, this is like finding δ that lowers active peaks while keeping others. If no negative δ? We can allow bounded and project.
This is a trust-region Newton step for a Least-Squares approximation of a constant target to the linearized convolution. Because the map a→c is quadratic but linearization around current can be good if δ small. We can use step size <1 and line search.

We can choose A as top K coefficients, target T = max(c_A) * (1 - reduction) or perhaps mean(c_A). If we try to fit all active to their mean, it flattens peaks.
Solve with Kx n matrix. For K≈50-200, n up to 2048, building A K*n okay. But solving (n×n) matrix n=2048 each iteration too expensive (8 million, okay maybe 0.1s? 2400s many). We can solve via normal equations with regularization and maybe use SVD? Better: Use conjugate gradient / iterative since A^T W A is n×n but can apply operator via FFT. But n=512, dense solve fine. For n=2048, 2048³ ~8.6e9 too heavy. We can restrict to n≤1024 for Gauss-Newton or use iterative.

Alternatively form K×n and compute δ via weighted least squares with constraints: δ = (A^T W A + μI)^{-1} (-A^T W r). This is Gram n×n. For n=1024, solve 1024x1024 ~1M operations? Actually LU ~n³/3=3.4e8, maybe 0.3s in numpy? Could be okay for few hundred iterations. For n=4096 too heavy. Use active Gauss-Newton for n≤1024, mirror for larger.

Another method: Since c = conv(a,a), we can view in frequency. Want linear convolution coefficients flat. The squared DFT magnitude relates to cyclic convolution of zero-padded a. If we pad length N power of two ≥2n, the zero-padded circular convolution of a with a is c. Its DFT is |A(ω)|². The coefficients c are inverse DFT of |A|². We want inverse DFT of |A|² to be flat (bounded). Thus |A(ω)|² should be as flat as possible across DFT bins, because if |A|² flat, c flat. But A is DFT of nonnegative a. Can we choose a nonnegative such that |A(ω)|² nearly flat? This is exactly designing a polynomial with nearly constant magnitude on unit circle (low distortion). Let's think about exact local optimum conditions. For a (positive interior), with active peaks A. Lagrangian: minimize max c. At optimum there exist weights λ_k≥0 on active peaks with ∑λ=1 such that gradient of ∑λ c_k is constant on support (for sum constraint) and zero? For positive all n, derivative condition: ∑λ_k 2a_{k-i} (diag half) = μ for all i. This means a is a "replicator" of the active peak weight distribution. This is like an eigenvector of a matrix defined by active peaks. We can use this to refine: Given current active peaks, find weights λ making the gradient as flat as possible; then a should satisfy `cross(A_active, λ) ≈ constant`. We can solve λ (nonnegative) to match a given a_i values? This is an inverse problem. Maybe use EM style: choose weights λ on peaks to make subgradient balanced, then update a proportional to? Hmm.

At optimum, all positive coordinates have equal m_i = ∑_{k active} λ_k 2a_{k-i}. Since a_i unknown. This is nonlinear. Could iterate: given a, compute m_i, choose λ to minimize variance of m_i? Then update a_i ∝ ? Maybe.

Alternatively, use "mirror descent" with λ weights chosen as softmax of c. The gradient l_i = ∑ p_k 2a_{k-i}. If p is optimal weights, l_i should be constant. The multiplicative update a_i ← a_i exp(-η l_i) in log? If l_i high reduce. At optimum l_i constant? For exact max with p on active peaks, gradient constant. Smooth mirror descent will naturally drive l_i constant under updates? Since a update changes l.

This suggests the mirror descent on θ with p=softmax(c) is essentially chasing equilibrium. Previous top-K subgradient is a hard version. We can improve by using smooth p and many iterations.

Could use alternating minimization of c_k: For each k, to reduce c_k, scale a_i on diagonal i+j=k by factor <1, but that affects others. Use exponential updates based on c_k values: a_i ← a_i ∏_k exp(-η_k something). This is same gradient.

Maybe use **Nesterov accelerated mirror descent** with smooth objective. We need good step size. Let's implement robust adaptive.

Function `smooth_mirror_search(a, budget, beta_start, beta_end, seed)`:
- Maintain theta? We can maintain a directly and do log updates.
- Use Adam on log a variables with raw gradient d (or l). Since we normalize, log variables y=log a (not normalized). Normalize after each update.
Pseudo:
```
a = normalize(a)
y = log(a) # maybe use directly a multiplicative
m = zeros; v = zeros
lr = n *? (since gradient scale)
beta = lambda/n
for it:
    c=conv(a,a)
    # anneal lambda
    l = current lambda
    p = softmax(l*c/n)
    d = 2*cross(p,a) - diag
    # gradient in log a: d? We can use d centered by weighted mean maybe before clipping.
    g = d - np.dot(a,d)/np.dot(a,a)? or d - mean(d)
    # Adam
    m = b1*m + (1-b1)*g
    v = b2*v + (1-b2)*g*g
    y -= lr * m/(sqrt(v)+eps)
    a_new = normalize(exp(y)) # or a*exp(-lr*...)
    # line search maybe if smooth loss not improving reduce lr
```
But using y and exp each time can overflow; maintain a and update times exp(-lr*grad) and normalize, also maintain log scale? For Adam, if we maintain y, okay; subtract max before exp.
Scale lr: g=d~0.5. To move a significantly, lr ~0.1. Adam divides by RMS ~0.5, so effective step ~lr. Good.
Window: since objective scale n, maybe lr ~1.
We can adapt lr: after step, if smooth_loss (with current λ) > previous, reduce lr; if improves, increase slightly.
But smooth loss changes with λ; use current λ.

However mirror descent on nonconvex smooth may oscillate. Use Nesterov momentum instead of Adam maybe.

Maybe simpler: Use `scipy.optimize.minimize` L-BFGS-B on y with normalization inside? Let's explore. We can define objective and gradient in terms of y where a = normalize(exp(y)). But normalize exp(y) uses softmax; derivative as above. This is smooth. L-BFGS-B can find local minima. We can set maxiter ~200 and callback time. It may be very efficient. Starting from multiple profiles and annealed λ. Let's consider implementing because it uses robust line search and limited memory.

For n=512, L-BFGS-B 200 iterations, each gradient FFT O(512 log) plus Python overhead, maybe <10s. Good. For n=2048, 200 iterations maybe >30s. We can run few.

But L-BFGS-B on nonconvex high-dim may struggle. We can use it as final polish for small n and parametric optimization.

Let's derive L-BFGS-B objective/gradient in terms of y with `a = n * softmax(y)`.
Loss L(y) = (2/n)*(1/β)*logsumexp(β c(a)). c depends on a.
Gradient g_y = a * (l - μ), where l = (2/n)*d, μ = dot(a,l)/n. Wait check: Earlier ∂a_i/∂θ_j = a_i(δ_ij/n - a_j/n²). If we use θ=y and a=n softmax(y), derivative = a_i(δ_ij - a_j/n)/n? Let's recompute carefully.
Let p_i = e_i / Z = a_i/n. ∂p_j/∂θ_i = p_j(δ_ij - p_i). a_j = n p_j. ∂a_j/∂θ_i = n ∂p_j/∂θ_i = a_j(δ_ij - p_i) = a_j(δ_ij - a_i/n).
Then grad_θ_i = ∑_j grad_a_j ∂a_j/∂θ_i = a_i grad_a_i - (a_i/n) ∑_j grad_a_j a_j = a_i (grad_a_i - μ), where μ = (1/n)∑ a_j grad_a_j. Yes. Good.

Also use **Gauss-Newton** polishing. Let's design it.

Gauss-Newton active step:
Input a, active coefficients K (top peaks or all above threshold). Build derivative matrix D of size K×n, row k: derivative of c_k wrt a. We can build using slicing:
For each k in ks:
  start=max(0,k-(n-1)); stop=min(n,k+1)
  idx = np.arange(start,stop)
  vals = a[k-idx]  # length
  row[idx] = 2*vals
  di = k - idx
  # where di == idx (k=2i), subtract vals (so one instead of two)
  mask = (idx == k - idx)
  row[idx[mask]] -= vals[mask]
This matches d row.
Then linearized c' = c_k + D δ. We want reduce peaks toward target T. Use weighted least squares:
If target T = maybe current mean active or current max - delta. Let r = c_k - T. Solve (D^T W D + μ I + ν 11^T?) δ = -D^T W r. To preserve sum, we can add penalty ν(1^Tδ)^2 or solve with Lagrange. Easier: after solve, remove mean: δ -= mean(δ) (or weighted?) and optionally bound.
Since D entries scale a~1, D^T D n×n. Use μ = rho * trace(D^T D)/n to regularize. Solve with np.linalg.solve. For n up to 1024, okay.
Step δ, choose step_size by line search: a_candidate = normalize(a + α δ) with α small (e.g. 0.1) and nonnegative (if any a+αδ<0, cap α = min(a_i/(-δ_i))). Compute exact max; accept if improves best/current.
Target selection: To reduce max, set T = current exact max * (1 - 0.01) or maybe median of active coefficients. If T too low, solution may increase others. Use T = mean(c_active) maybe to flatten active peaks. Since max active > mean, fitting to mean reduces peaks. But may raise lows. Use active K top ~max(16, n//20). Weight w = softmax(λ_local c_active/lam?) or w=1. Weighted by excess over T: w = exp(λ(c_k - T)/scale). But target and weights interplay. Simpler: choose K top, weights all 1, T = np.mean(c_k) of active? Maybe set T = np.quantile(c, 0.9)??? Let's think.

def smooth_search(a, budget, seed=0, lam_start=5, lam_end=80, lr0=0.15):
    a = normalize(a)
    n=a.size
    rng=...
    t0=time.monotonic(); deadline=t0+budget
    y = np.log(a)  # not normalized? We'll use a multiplicative direct maybe.
    # Use y and softmax each eval? If maintain a directly and y for perturb? For exact gradient, need y. But we can update y and recompute a = n*softmax(y).
    # To avoid overflow, softmax(y - max(y)).
    y = np.asarray(np.log(a + 1e-300), dtype)
    m = zeros; v=zeros? Use Nesterov: v = zeros
    mu_mom=0.9
    lr=lr0
    best_a=a.copy(); best_score=fast_score(a)
    it=0; no_improve=0
    # lam schedule: linear or exponential over iterations/time. Use time fraction.
    while time<deadline:
        frac = (time-t0)/budget
        lam = lam_start * (lam_end/lam_start)**frac  # exp
        a = normalize_softmax(y,n)
        c = conv(a,a)
        maxc = c.max()
        score = 2*maxc/n
        if score < best_score: best...
        # if best below target maybe increase lam, reduce lr?
        l, lgrad = loss_grad_softmax(y,n,lam) # recomputes c
        # Nesterov: v = mu*v - lr*(lgrad - maybe center? lgrad sum zero)
        v = 0.8*v - lr*lgrad
        y_new = y + v
        # line search? Instead of full line search, use trial loss
        a_new = normalize_softmax(y_new,n)
        c_new = conv(a_new,a_new)
        score_new = 2*maxc_new/n
        # accept if score improves or smooth loss improves? Use exact score to accept because final; but score nonsmooth. Use soft loss maybe.
```

But Nesterov velocity compels line search. Maybe simpler: use gradient with backtracking no momentum:
```
while:
  loss,grad=...
  step=lr
  for tries in range(5):
     y_t = y - step*grad   # (grad is gradient of soft loss, not scaled)
     loss_t=loss(y_t)
     if loss_t < loss:
         y=y_t; break
     step*=0.5
  else: lr*=0.5
```
This may be slow but stable. Add momentum after stable? We can use Adam.

```
def soft_loss_y(y, n, lam, tol=None):
    a = n*softmax(y)
    c=conv(a,a)
    m=c.max(); ex=np.exp((lam/n)*(c-m)); W=ex.sum()
    loss = m + (n/lam)*np.log(W)  # = logZ/β
    return loss
```
Derivative as above but need d and μ. We can implement `grad_y(y,n,lam)`.

def smooth_mirror_step(theta,n,lam):
    a = softmax_n(theta,n)
    c, p, loss = conv_and_weights(a,n,lam)
    d = gradient_d(a,p,c? only diag)
    mu = dot(a,d)/n
    g = a*(d-mu)
    return loss, g, c.max()
```

`convolution_c(a)`:
Use N based on a size? For c only, N=1 << ((2*n-1)-1).bit_length(). But for combined, use N=1 << ((3*n-2)-1).bit_length(). Implement combined to reduce.
```
def c_and_p_and_d(a, lam):
   n=a.size; L=2*n-1
   N=1 << (3*n-2 -1).bit_length()  # if 3n-2 power? bit_length of m-1
   fa=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
   c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa,N)[:L]
   np.maximum(c,0,out=c)
   beta=lam/float(n)
   m=float(c.max())
   arg=beta*(c-m)
   # arg max 0. If m huge? c~n, lam/n => lam. okay.
   ex=np.exp(arg); W=ex.sum()
   p=ex/W
   a_rev=a[::-1]
   fp=np.fft.rfft(p,N)
   fr=np.fft.rfft(a_rev,N)
   convpa=np.fft.irfft(fp*fr,N)
   cross=convpa[n-1:2*n-1]
   # due to numerical, cross may slightly negative; no? p,a positive.
   diag=p[0::2]*a  # length n? p[0:2n-1:2] length n
   d=2*cross - diag
   loss=m + (float(n)/lam)*np.log(W)  # beta=lam/n => 1/beta=n/lam
   return c, p, d, loss, m
```
Need ensure N for p*a_rev: length L+n-1 = 3n-2, correct. `irfft` returns length N; slice `n-1` to `2*n-1` inclusive? Need length n: indices n-1,...,2n-2. So `convpa[n-1:2*n-1]` yes length n.
Check p[0::2]: p length 2n-1, indices 0,2,...,2n-2 length n.

But `loss = m + (n/lam)*logW`: If lam high, logW~log(K), loss~m. Good. If lamb very small, logW~logL, n/lam large; loss may huge? Actually as β→0, logZ=β mean + β²..., logZ/β=mean + ...; formula m + logW/β. def smooth_search(a, budget, seed=0, lam_start=8, lam_end=200):
    a=normalize_sum(a)
    n=a.size
    if budget<=0: return a, score_fast(a)
    rng=...
    # initial theta: from a, set last 0: theta=log(a[:n-1]/a[-1]) if a[-1]>0 else -10.
    def init_theta(x): ... top-level? Need function `make_theta(x,n)`
    theta=make_theta(a,n)
    v? Use Adam? Let's implement a robust one:
       m_adam=0; v_adam=0; t=0
       lr=0.5; b1=0.9; b2=0.999; eps=1e-8
       best_a=a; best_score=score_fast(a)
       stall=0
       last_lam=lam_start
       y=theta.copy()
       # time loop
       while time<deadline:
          frac=(time-t0)/budget
          lam = lam_start * (lam_end/lam_start)**frac
          # If lam change large, maybe reset momentum? if lam > last_lam*2: v*=0.5
          a = softmax_theta(theta,n)
          c,p,d,loss,m = c_and_p_and_d(a,lam)
          score=2*m/n (since sum n)
          if score<best_score: best...
          # gradient g = a*(d - dot(a,d)/n)
          mu=np.dot(a,d)/n
          g=a*(d-mu)
          if not finite: reset
          t+=1
          m_adam=b1*m_adam+(1-b1)*g
          v_adam=b2*v_adam+(1-b2)*g*g
          mhat=m_adam/(1-b1**t); vhat=v_adam/(1-b2**t)
          step=lr*mhat/(np.sqrt(vhat)+eps)
          # Clip step to avoid huge
          step=np.clip(step,-3,3)
          theta_trial=theta - step
          a_trial=softmax_theta(theta_trial,n)
          c_trial,p_trial,d_trial,loss_trial,m_trial=c_and_p_and_d(a_trial,lam)
          score_trial=2*m_trial/n
          if loss_trial < loss*1.0? and score_trial <= score*(1+0.05) and finite:
              theta=theta_trial
              # adapt lr
              if score_trial < best_score: stall=0; lr=min(lr*1.02,2.0)
              else: stall+=1
          else:
              lr=max(0.05, lr*0.7)
              stall+=1
          # if stall high, add noise/restart from best
          if stall > 80:
              # smooth restart: take best, add small noise to theta
              base_a=best_a.copy(); perturb...
              theta=make_theta(perturb,n)
              m_adam=v_adam=0; lr=0.5; lam = max(lam_start, lam*0.5?) # maybe reset to lower lam to escape
              stall=0
              best_score? 
          print every 20s
```

def theta_to_a(theta, n):
    w = np.empty(n); w[:-1]=theta; w[-1]=0
    w -= w.max()
    e = np.exp(w); s=e.sum(); if s==0: s=1
    return n*e/s

```
def random_smooth_profile(n,rng,M=8):
   t=(arange(n)+0.5)/n
   z=zero
   for m in 1..M:
      amp=rng.normal(0, 1.0/math.sqrt(m))
      ph=rng.uniform(0,2π)
      z += amp*np.sin(2π*m*t+ph)
   # Maybe add small cos to allow non-antisymmetric
   a=np.exp(z*0.7)
   return normalize_sum(a,n)
```

       # Build D
       D = np.empty((K,n))
       for row,k in enumerate(idx): ...
       # Regularization and solve
       G = D @ D.T
       mu = 1e-6 * float(np.trace(G)/K) + 1e-12
       y = T - ck  # desired change
       # If y all zero, break
       try: gamma = np.linalg.solve(G + mu*np.eye(K), y)
       except: gamma = np.linalg.lstsq(G, y, rcond=None)[0]
       delta = D.T @ gamma
       delta -= np.mean(delta)
       # scale if large: maximize step such that a+δ positive? Use α search.
       if delta.max abs <1e-12: break

           max_alpha_scale = min(0.5, float(np.min(a[neg] / (-delta[neg])))*0.9)

       for alpha in [max_alpha_scale, max_alpha_scale*0.5, *0.25, *0.1, *0.05? but if max=0.5, okay]
           if alpha<=0: continue
           cand=a+alpha*delta
           if cand.min()< -1e-12: continue
           cand=np.clip(cand,0,None)
           cand=normalize_sum(cand,n)
           sc=score_fast(cand)
           if finite and sc<best_trial_score:
               best_trial_score=sc; best_alpha=alpha; best_cand=cand
       if best_alpha>0 and best_trial_score < best_score:
           a=best_cand; best_score=best_trial_score
           # maybe print
       else:
           # reduce K or target? If no improve, break or perturb.
           K = max(16, K//2) if K>16 else K
           if K<16: break
           # optional add small random perturbation
```

GLOBAL_MIN_STATE = {}
def minimize_callback(x):
    st = GLOBAL_MIN_STATE
    if time.monotonic() >= st['deadline']:
        return True
    st['calls'] += 1
    now=time.monotonic()
    if st['calls'] % 5 == 0 or now >= st['next_update']:
        a = theta_to_a(x, st['n'], st['ref'])
        sc = score_fast(a)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < st['best_score']:
            st['best_score']=sc; st['best_a']=a.copy()
        st['next_update']=now+0.5
    return False
```

`smooth_perturb` implementation:
```
def smooth_perturb(a, rng, strength=0.3):
   n=a.size; S=target_sum_for_length(n)
   a=normalize_sum(a,S)
   # theta with ref=argmax
   ref=...; theta=make_theta(a,ref)
   if len(theta)>2:
      # add smooth Fourier noise to a multiplicatively:
      t=(np.arange(n)+0.5)/n
      z=0
      for m in 1..3: amp=strength/(m+1); ph=rng.uniform(0,2π); z += amp*np.cos(2π*m*t+ph)
      a *= np.exp(z)
      # maybe local noise block
      if rng.random()<0.4:
         width=max(1,n//50); start=...; a[start:start+width]*=np.exp(rng.normal(0,strength,width))
   a=normalize_sum(a,S)
   return a
```

def run_length(n, budget, global_best, global_score, seed, parent):
   if budget < 30: return upsample global best, score
   t0=...; deadline=t0+budget
   S=target_sum_for_length(n)
   starts=generate_starts(...)
   # Keep top up to 4, but at least first global.
   starts.sort(key...); top=starts[:4]
   best_a=top[0][1]; best_score=top[0][0]
   # If best_score not finite, fallback uniform
   # Allocate smooth time fractions with overhead for GN.
   # Let smooth_frac = 0.65, gn_frac=0.25, restart_frac=0.10
   # For top starts, allocate smooth time proportional to (1/score?) but at least fraction.
   # Simpler: run best start with 0.7*smooth_budget, second with 0.3 if time.
   smooth_budget = 0.62*budget
   gn_budget = 0.25*budget
   restart_budget = budget - smooth_budget - gn_budget
   # But if multiple starts, use fraction of smooth:
   start_alloc = [0.6,0.25,0.15] for top available.
   for idx,start in enumerate(top):
       time_this = smooth_budget * alloc[idx]
       if time_this < 20: continue
       a = start
       a, sc = annealed_lbf(a, time_this, seed+idx)
       if sc<best_score: update
   # GN on best
   a, sc = gauss_newton_polish(best_a, gn_budget, seed+100)
   update
   # Restarts using remaining
   if restart_budget>30:
       for r in range(2):
           time_each=restart_budget/2
           a_pert=smooth_perturb(best_a, rng, 0.4)
           a,sc=annealed_lbf(a_pert,time_each*0.7, seed+1000+r)
           update
           a,sc=gauss_newton_polish(best_a,time_each*0.3, seed+2000+r)
           update
   return best_a,best_score
```

def run_lbf_phase(theta, n, ref, S, lam, budget, a_now, score_now):
    if budget < 5: return a_now, score_now, theta
    MIN_STATE.clear()
    MIN_STATE['deadline'] = time.monotonic()+budget
    MIN_STATE['n']=n; MIN_STATE['ref']=ref; MIN_STATE['S']=S
    MIN_STATE['best_score']=score_now; MIN_STATE['best_a']=a_now.copy()
    MIN_STATE['calls']=0; MIN_STATE['next_update']=time.monotonic()
    bounds=[(-80.0,80.0)]*(n-1)
    options={'maxiter': 2000, 'maxfun': 200000, 'maxls': 20, 'ftol':1e-12, 'gtol':1e-10}
    try:
       res=optimize.minimize(loss_from_theta, theta, jac=grad_from_theta, method='L-BFGS-B', args=(n,lam,ref,S), bounds=bounds, callback=minimize_callback, options=options)
    except Exception:
       res=None
    # after, final theta maybe res.x
    best_a=MIN_STATE['best_a']; best_score=MIN_STATE['best_score']
    # evaluate final if finite
    if res is not None and len(res.x)==n-1:
       a_f=theta_to_a(res.x,n,ref,S); sc=score_fast(a_f)
       if finite and sc<best_score: best...
    return best_a,best_score,res.x if res else theta
```

`target_sum_for_length(n)`:
```
def target_sum_for_length(n):
    if n <= 0: return 1.0
    return float(min(int(n), 1000))
```
If n is float? pass int.

`normalize_sum(a,S)`:
```
def normalize_sum(a,S=None):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64).copy()
    if a.size==0:
        return np.ones(1)
    if S is None: S=target_sum_for_length(a.size)
    a=np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a=np.clip(a,0.0,1000.0)
    s=float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-14:
        return np.full(a.size, S/float(a.size))
    a *= S/s
    # if due to rounding max>1000? since S<=1000 and nonneg sum S, max<=S<=1000, but rounding maybe
    if a.size>0 and a[0]...:
       a=np.clip(a,0.0,1000.0)
       s2=float(a.sum())
       if s2>0: a *= S/s2
    return a
```
If S=1000,n=1, max=1000; okay. If S≤1000, nonnegative sum S ensures max≤S mathematically, but due to rounding maybe a.max()=1000.0000000002; clip/rescale.

`upsample_sequence(seq,n)`:
```
def upsample_sequence(seq,n):
    seq=np.asarray(seq,float)
    if seq.size==0: return np.ones(n)
    if seq.size==n: return normalize_sum(seq)
    xold=np.linspace(0,1,seq.size); xnew=np.linspace(0,1,n)
    out=np.interp(xnew,xold,seq)
    return normalize_sum(out)
```
If n=1, linspace okay.

`conv_c(a)`:
```
def conv_c(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,float)
    n=a.size
    if n==0: return np.empty(0)
    L=2*n-1
    N=1 << (L-1).bit_length()
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
    c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa,N)[:L]
    c=np.ascontiguousarray(c)
    np.maximum(c,0.0,out=c)
    return c
```
If N=1 for n=1, rfft length1, okay.

`score_fast(a)`:
```
def score_fast(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,float)
    if a.size==0: return inf
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(a)): return inf
    S=float(a.sum())
    if S < 0.01: return inf
    c=conv_c(a)
    if c.size==0: return inf
    m=float(np.max(c))
    return float(2.0*a.size*m/(S*S))
```

`c_p_d(a,lam,S=None)`:
```
def c_p_d(a,lam,S=None):
   a=np.asarray(a,float)
   n=a.size
   if S is None: S=float(a.sum())
   if n==0: ...
   if S<=0: S=1.0
   L=2*n-1
   N=1 << (L-1).bit_length()
   # For cross need N2 maybe >N. Use N2 for both to avoid two? Simpler: use N2 = nextpow2(3n-2), do both there.
   N=1 << (3*n-2 -1).bit_length() if n>1 else 1
   fa=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
   c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa,N)[:L]; clip
   peak_scale=(S*S)/float(n) if n>0 else 1.0
   if peak_scale <=1e-12: peak_scale=1.0
   beta=float(lam)/peak_scale
   m=float(c.max())
   if m < 0: m=0
   arg=beta*(c-m)
   # To avoid overflow if beta range large? arg max 0, min negative. fine.
   ex=np.exp(arg)
   W=float(ex.sum())
   if W <=0 or not finite: W=1; p=c/L? 
   p=ex/W
   a_rev=a[::-1]
   fp=np.fft.rfft(p,N)
   fr=np.fft.rfft(a_rev,N)
   convpa=np.fft.irfft(fp*fr,N)
   cross=convpa[n-1:2*n-1]
   cross=np.maximum(cross,0.0)
   diag=p[0::2]*a  # length n if L=2n-1
   d=2.0*cross - diag
   d=np.maximum(d,0.0)
   loss = m + float(np.log(W))/beta
   return c,p,d,loss
```

`loss_from_theta`:
```
def loss_from_theta(theta,n,lam,ref,S):
   a=theta_to_a(theta,n,ref,S)
   c=conv_c(a)
   if not finite: return 1e100
   beta = lam / ((S*S)/n)
   m=c.max()
   arg=beta*(c-m)
   W=sum(exp)
   loss=m+logW/beta
   return float(loss) if finite else 1e100
```
No score. Good.

Now, `annealed_lbf` phases. Let's define:
```
def annealed_lbf(a,budget,seed=0):
    n=a.size; S=target_sum_for_length(n)
    a=normalize_sum(a,S)
    best_a=a; best_score=score_fast(a)
    if budget<20: return best_a,best_score
    t0=time.monotonic(); deadline=t0+budget
    phases=[(10,0.18),(20,0.27),(40,0.30),(80,0.18),(160,0.07)]
    # If budget small, use fewer high phases: maybe scale.
    total_time = budget-5
    for lam,frac in phases:
       tt=total_time*frac
       if time.monotonic()>=deadline-2: break
       if tt<10: continue
       # choose ref, theta
       ref=int(np.argmax(a))
       theta=make_theta(a,ref)
       # maybe if a is best, but a may have changed; use a not best_a? We should start from current a (best from previous phase)
       a_new, score_new, theta_new = run_lbf_phase(theta,n,ref,S,lam,tt,a,best_score? score_fast(a))
       if score_new < best_score:
           best_a=a_new; best_score=score_new
       # Even if score not improved, maybe a_new soft improved but exact not; keep best_a to avoid degradation.
       # For next phase, use best_a.
    return best_a,best_score
```
Should we pass current a or best_a? Use best_a to avoid drift. But if current a has better soft landscape for next λ but exact worse, maybe could lead to better later. To be safe, use best_a for exact objective. Could also use `a_new` if its score is within 0.5% of best. Maybe:
```
if score_new < best_score * 1.001: a = a_new else a = best_a
```
But exact best likely.

If budget very large, phases short; good.

`run_lbf_phase` returns best_a possibly initial.

Pseudo refined:
```
def gauss_newton_polish(a, budget, seed=0):
    n=a.size; S=target_sum_for_length(n); a=normalize_sum(a,S)
    best_a=a.copy(); best_score=score_fast(a)
    t0=...; deadline=t0+budget
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    K = min(2*n-1, max(48, n//8)); K=min(K,256)
    # Maybe for n small K=64, for 4096 K=256.
    no_improve=0
    while time<deadline-1:
       c=conv_c(a); maxc=c.max(); Sact=a.sum(); score=2*n*maxc/(Sact*Sact)
       if score<best_score: best...
       if K > 2*n-1: K=...
       idx=np.argpartition(c, -K)[-K:] if K<2*n-1 else np.arange(2*n-1)
       ck=c[idx]
       # build D once
       D=np.empty((K,n))
       for row,k in enumerate(idx): ...
       G=D @ D.T
       mean_diag=float(np.trace(G)/K) if K>0 else 1.0
       mu=1e-6*mean_diag + 1e-12
       eye=np.eye(K)
       # targets
       targets=[]
       mean_ck=float(np.mean(ck))
       maxk=float(np.max(ck))
       targets.append(mean_ck)
       targets.append(maxk - 0.002*n?) Wait c units: S²/n. Use drop fraction of maxk: maxk*(1-0.005) and (1-0.01). Also maybe global average = (Sact*Sact)/(2*n-1). But active mean enough.
       targets.append(maxk*0.995); targets.append(max( mean_ck, maxk*0.99))
       unique targets within 1e-6.
       best_delta=None; best_trial=score
       for T in targets:
           y=T-ck
           if np.max(np.abs(y)) < 1e-8*max(1,maxk): continue
           try: gamma=np.linalg.solve(G+mu*eye, y)
           except: gamma=np.linalg.lstsq(G,y,rcond=None)[0]
           if not finite: continue
           delta=D.T @ gamma
           delta-=np.mean(delta)
           if not finite or delta.max()>delta.min? continue
           # line search
           pos_limit=1.0
           neg=delta<0; if any: pos_limit=min(pos_limit, min(a[neg]/(-delta[neg]))*0.9)
           for alpha in [1.0,0.5,0.25,0.125,0.0625]:
               aa=alpha*pos_limit
               if aa<=1e-12: continue
               cand=a+aa*delta
               if cand.min()< -1e-9: continue
               cand=np.clip(cand,0,None)
               cand=normalize_sum(cand,S)
               sc=score_fast(cand)
               if finite and sc<best_trial:
                   best_trial=sc; best_delta=delta.copy(); best_alpha=aa; best_cand=cand
       if best_cand is not None and best_trial < best_score:
           a=best_cand; best_score=best_trial; no_improve=0
       else:
           no_improve+=1
           K=max(24, K*3//4)
           if no_improve>6 or K<24: break
    return best_a,best_score
```
Targets: c units ~S²/n. For n>1000,S=1000, uniform max=488; target drop 0.5% ~2.44. Good. `maxk*0.995` and `0.99`. For n=512,S=512, uniform max=512; target mean top maybe near. Good.
Maybe target `Sact*Sact/(2*n-1)` (global average) too low; not.
Add target `np.quantile(ck, 0.5)` maybe equal mean. already.
If active K includes max and many near max, mean ~max, targets 0.995 more aggressive. Good.
Regularization μ: If G ill-conditioned, solve maybe large. Use `np.linalg.solve`; if LinAlgError, use `lstsq`.
`eye` allocate K×K each loop; K 256 okay. Could reuse but fine.
Line search `pos_limit=1.0`. If δ has large positive and negative, cap by negative. If no negative, α up to1. But δ may have large positive causing sum normalized; okay. Could also cap if `a + delta > 1000`? Since S≤1000 and nonnegative, a+δ before normalize could exceed 1000 huge; normalize rescales. The gradient step could be nonsensical if raw huge. We can scale δ to have norm relative to a: e.g., `delta_norm = np.linalg.norm(delta) / np.linalg.norm(a)`. If >10, `delta /= delta_norm`? But line search α up to1 might still too big. We can set `delta = delta / (np.max(np.abs(delta)) + 1e-12)`? Then α represents units. Better: Keep δ raw but if `max_abs = max(abs(delta),1e-12); scale = S/max_abs`; if scale<1, δ*=scale? This makes max δ ≤S, α up to1 changes at most 100%. Could be okay. Actually raw δ from LS may be already similar. To be safe, after centering:
```
maxabs=np.max(np.abs(delta))
if maxabs > S:
    delta *= S/maxabs
    pos_limit=1.0
elif maxabs > 0:

`theta_to_a`: If `S <=0`, return ones. `w` may have nonfinite; replace with -100. Then subtract max. If e sum nan, fallback.
```
def theta_to_a(theta,n,ref,S):
   w=expand_theta(theta,n,ref)
   w=np.where(np.isfinite(w),w,-100.0)
   wmax=float(np.max(w)) if n>0 else 0
   e=np.exp(w-wmax)
   s=float(e.sum())
   if s <=0 or not finite:
       a=np.full(n,S/float(n))
   else:
       a=S*e/s
   return a
```
Since S≤1000, mean S/n.

`expand_theta` for n=1: theta length0; w length1. If ref=0, w[0]=0. Works if we handle ref==0 before `w[1:]`.
```
def expand_theta(theta,n,ref):
    w=np.empty(n)
    if n==1:
       w[0]=0; return w
    if ref==0:
       w[0]=0; w[1:]=theta
    elif ref==n-1:
       w[:-1]=theta; w[-1]=0
    else:
       w[:ref]=theta[:ref]; w[ref]=0; w[ref+1:]=theta[ref:]
    return w
```
If ref maybe out of bounds? clamp.
`make_theta` returns length n-1. If n==1, returns empty.

`remove_ref` similar:
```
def remove_ref(v,n,ref):
   if n==1: return np.empty(0)
   if ref==0: return v[1:]
   if ref==n-1: return v[:-1]
   out=np.empty(n-1); out[:ref]=v[:ref]; out[ref:]=v[ref+1:]; return out
```

    a=a.copy(); a[ref]=1e-6; a=normalize_sum(a,target_sum_for_length(n))
base=a/a[ref]

w[ref]=0

theta=np.where(np.isfinite(theta),theta,-100.0)

```
If a[ref] max, base≤1, log≤0. Good.

Fallback function `simple_smooth_phase(theta,n,ref,S,lam,budget,a_init,score_init)`:
```
y=theta.copy(); best_a=a_init; best_score=score_init
lr=0.3; stall=0
deadline=...
while time<deadline:
   a=theta_to_a(y,n,ref,S)
   c,p,d,loss=c_and_p_and_d(a,lam,S)
   score=2*n*(c.max())/(S*S)
   update best
   mu=dot(a,d)/S
   g=a*(d-mu)
   g=remove_ref(g,n,ref)
   if not finite: break
   # backtracking
   step=lr
   improved=False
   for _ in range(5):
      y_trial=y - step*g
      a_trial=theta_to_a(y_trial,n,ref,S)
      c_trial,_,_,loss_trial=c_and_p_and_d(a_trial,lam,S)
      if loss_trial < loss*(1+1e-12):
          y=y_trial; improved=True; break
      step*=0.5
   if improved:
      lr=min(1.0,lr*1.05); stall=0
   else:
      lr=max(0.05,lr*0.7); stall+=1
      if stall>60: break
return best
```

Now, `run_length` allocations:
```
def run_length(n,budget,global_best,global_score,seed,parent):
   if budget < 20:
      if global_best is None: seq=np.ones(n)
      else: seq=upsample_sequence(global_best,n)
      return seq, score_fast(seq)
   deadline=time.monotonic()+budget
   starts=generate_starts(n,global_best,parent,seed, random_count=20 if n>2048 else 35)
   if not starts: ...
   best_a,start_score = starts[0]
   # Maybe top 3 starts
   top=starts[:3]
   # Allocate smooth_time by score? Use fractions [0.6,0.3,0.1]
   smooth_budget = 0.55*budget
   gn_budget = 0.30*budget
   restart_budget = max(0,budget-smooth_budget-gn_budget)
   allocations=[0.55,0.30,0.15]
   for i,(sc,seq) in enumerate(top):
       if i>=len(allocations): break
       time_this = smooth_budget*allocations[i]
       if time.monotonic() >= deadline-30: break
       if time_this < 15: continue
       a_new,sc_new=annealed_lbf(seq,time_this,seed+i*10)
       if sc_new < start_score:
           start_score=sc_new; best_a=a_new
       if sc_new < global_score:
           global_score=sc_new; global_best=a_new
   # GN on best_a
   if time.monotonic() < deadline-10:
       a_gn,sc_gn=gauss_newton_polish(best_a, gn_budget, seed+1000)
       if sc_gn < global_score: update
   # Restarts
   if time.monotonic() < deadline-20 and restart_budget>20:
       rng=np.random.default_rng(seed+777)
       num=max(1,int(restart_budget/60))
       each=remaining/num
       for r in range(num):
          a_p=smooth_perturb(global_best, rng, 0.35)
          t_smooth=each*0.65
          if t_smooth>15:
             a_r,sc_r=annealed_lbf(a_p,t_smooth,seed+5000+r)
             if sc_r<global_score: update
          t_gn=each*0.35
          if t_gn>15:
             a_r,sc_r=gauss_newton_polish(global_best,t_gn,seed+6000+r)
             if sc_r<global_score: update
   return global_best,global_score
```
Need be careful: `global_best` and `global_score` passed by value; updating inside local variables; return.

`smooth_perturb` if `a` None return None.
```
def smooth_perturb(a,rng,strength=0.3):
   if a is None: return np.ones(512)
   n=a.size; S=target_sum_for_length(n); a=normalize_sum(a,S)
   t=(np.arange(n)+0.5)/n
   z=zeros
   for m in (1,2,3,4): amp=strength/(m+1); ph=rng.uniform(0,2π); z += amp*np.cos(2π*m*t+ph)
   a *= np.exp(z)
   if rng.random()<0.4:
       width=max(1,n//40); start=rng.integers(0,n-width+1); a[start:start+width]*=np.exp(rng.normal(0,strength,width))
   return normalize_sum(a,S)
```
No closures.

Now, let's revisit gradient derivation to be 100% sure. Convolution c_k = ∑_{i=0}^{n-1}∑_{j=0}^{n-1} a_i a_j 1_{i+j=k}. Derivative wrt a_t = ∑_{i+j=k} (δ_{i,t} a_j + δ_{j,t} a_i). If i=t, j=k-t; if valid, contribution a_{k-t}. If j=t, i=k-t; if valid, contribution a_t. For k-t valid. If t=k-t, the pair (t,t) appears once in double sum? In double sum over i,j, pair (t,t) appears once (i=t,j=t). The derivative of term a_t*a_t is 2a_t. Our formula: i=t contribution a_t; j=t contribution a_t; but are these two separate? The double sum includes only one pair (t,t), but derivative of product gives 2a_t, equivalent to summing both. So formula works. If k-t invalid, no pair; derivative 0. Thus d_i = ∑_k p_k (a_i + a_{k-i})*1_{0≤k-i<n}. We rewrote as cross_i + a_i*q_i. cross_i =∑_{r=0}^{n-1}p_{i+r}a_r = second term? For i variable t, k-t = r; a_{k-t}=a_r. The second term from j=t is a_i =a_r? Wait for variable t=i, j=t gives i'=k-t=r, contribution a_{i'}? Let's map: For variable index t, pair j=t => first index i=k-t = r, contribution a_i = a_r. Pair i=t => contribution a_j = a_{k-t}=a_r. Both contributions are a_r! Ah, derivative = 2∑ p_k a_{k-i} for valid? Wait we earlier n=2 got correct with p0a0+p1a1 plus a0 window gave 2p0a0+2p1a1. Here q_i*a_i term? Let's recalc using this new understanding: For variable t, for each k valid r=k-t. Pair (i=t,j=r): derivative a_j=a_r. Pair (i=r,j=t): derivative a_i=a_r. If r≠t, there are two distinct pairs both with value a_r, total 2a_r. If r=t, pair (t,t) one term derivative 2a_t. Thus derivative = 2∑_{r=0}^{n-1} p_{i+r} a_r. That's exactly 2*cross_i! The q_i*a_i term was based on erroneously thinking second contribution a_i (wrong; it's a_{k-i}=a_r, not a_i). Let's verify n=2: cross0=p0a0+p1a1; d0=2cross0=2p0a0+2p1a1. correct. For n=3,k=2 central with variable i=1: cross1=∑_{r=0}^{2}p_{1+r}a_r with valid p1a0+p2a1+p3a2. d1=2cross1. Is derivative of central c2 wrt a1? c2=a0a2+a1²+a2a0. derivative wrt a1=2a1. p2 term in cross: p2*a1 *2=2p2a1. Good. Derivative wrt a0: cross0=p0a0+p1a1+p2a2; d0=2(...). c2 derivative wrt a0=2a2 (p2 term 2p2a2) plus other peaks. Good. So the original previous program's `2*cross` was actually correct! My "a_i + a_{k-i}" was mistaken because I used variable value a_i for second pair but second pair's other index is also k-i, same as mirror. Wait in pair (i=k-t, j=t), other index i = k-t = r, contribution a_i=a_r, not a_t. Yes. So derivative is 2cross, no q. Good! The previous heuristic gradient was true for weighted sums. Great. Our new correction would be wrong if d=cross+a_iq. Let's settle with a formal derivative: c_k = ∑_{j} a_j a_{k-j}. For variable a_i: terms where j=i: a_i a_{k-i} derivative a_{k-i}; terms where k-j=i => j=k-i: derivative a_i? Wait if j=k-i, term is a_{k-i} a_i, derivative wrt a_i is a_{k-i}, not a_i! Because term = a_j * a_{k-j} with j=k-i; derivative of a_{k-j}=a_i wrt a_i gives a_j = a_{k-i}. So both terms contribute a_{k-i}, not a_i. If j=i=k-i, one term a_i² derivative 2a_i. Thus derivative = 2 ∑_{valid} p_k a_{k-i}. Yes. So original formula 2*cross is correct. My "a_t" was wrong. Glad caught. The previous program's gradient_topK was correct (2*cross topK). Our `q` addition is wrong. Revert to d=2*cross. Need handle diagonal? For k=2i, cross includes p_{2i}a_i; 2cross gives 2p a_i, correct. No diagonal subtraction. Good.

Thus `d = 2*cross`. No prefix q. The earlier formula with cross+q double counts wrong. Let's use `d=2*cross`.

Why did I initially think previous program used correct? Yes it did `2*d` where d was average of a[j] for top peaks. Good. So no major correction needed. But our smooth gradient must be `2*cross` weighted by p. Let's update.

Check derivative of individual c_k: For variable i, derivative = 2 if mirror valid times a_{mirror}, including diagonal 2a_i. Yes.

So `c_and_p_and_d`: `d = 2.0 * cross` (clip >=0). No prefix. Much simpler.

GN D row for derivative of c_k wrt a_i = 2*a_{k-i} if valid, else 0. Previous program used `2*a[...]`, not `+a`. I earlier changed D to `a[idx]+a[k-idx]`, which is wrong. Revert to `2*a[k-idx]`. For diagonal, 2a_i correct. Good.

Let's prove with c_k double sum: ∑_{i+j=k} a_i a_j. Derivative wrt a_t: For pair (t,j) with j=k-t: derivative a_j. For pair (i,t) with i=k-t: derivative a_i. Both other index is k-t, so sum 2a_{k-t}. If t=k-t, the two pairs are the same? The double sum has one pair (t,t) but derivative of a_t^2=2a_t. The combinatorial argument of two contributions coincides. So formula 2a_{k-t}. Great.

Thus original previous gradient heuristic true. Our smooth true gradient uses d=2cross. Good.

def sanitize_for_return(seq):
    a=np.asarray(seq,float)
    if a.size==0: return [1.0]
    a=np.where(np.isfinite(a),a,0)
    a=np.clip(a,0.0,1000.0)
    S=float(a.sum())
    if S<0.01: return [1.0]*512
    # scale to sum min(n,1000) if possible; if max*target/S <=1000
    target=target_sum_for_length(a.size)
    scale=target/S
    if a.size>0 and a.max()*scale <= 1000.0:
       a*=scale
    else:
       a=np.clip(a,0,1000)
       S2=float(a.sum())
       if S2<0.01: return [1.0]*512
    return list(map(float,a))
```

```python
"""
Search for non-negative step-function heights minimizing
    2 * n * max(convolve(a, a)) / (sum(a))**2.

Strategy:
- Work with a normalized sequence a >= 0 whose sum is S = min(n, 1000),
  so the evaluator's 1000.0 cap is never reached.
- Parameterize a = S * softmax(theta) with one reference coordinate fixed.
- Minimize an annealed log-sum-exp surrogate of the peak convolution
  coefficient with L-BFGS-B and a true FFT-based gradient.
- Polish with active-set Gauss-Newton flattening of the largest convolution
  coefficients.
- Run several lengths, random smooth restarts, and final exact validation.
"""

import numpy as np
import time
import math

try:
    from scipy import optimize
    HAS_SCIPY = True
except Exception:
    HAS_SCIPY = False

MIN_STATE = {}
BOUND = 80.0


def _pair_score(item):
    return item[0]


def _next_pow2(m):
    m = int(max(1, m))
    if m <= 1:
        return 1
    return 1 << (m - 1).bit_length()


def target_sum_for_length(n):
    n = int(max(1, n))
    return float(min(n, 1000))


def normalize_sum(a, s_target=None):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    if s_target is None:
        s_target = target_sum_for_length(a.size)
    s_target = float(max(1e-9, s_target))
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if s <= 1e-14:
        return np.full(a.size, s_target / float(a.size), dtype=np.float64)
    a *= s_target / s
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s2 = float(np.sum(a))
    if s2 > 0.0:
        a *= s_target / s2
    return a


def upsample_sequence(seq, n):
    seq = np.asarray(seq, dtype=np.float64)
    n = int(max(1, n))
    if seq.size == 0:
        return normalize_sum(np.ones(n))
    if seq.size == n:
        return normalize_sum(seq)
    xold = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, seq.size)
    xnew = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    out = np.interp(xnew, xold, seq)
    return normalize_sum(out)


def conv_c(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)
    L = 2 * n - 1
    N = _next_pow2(L)
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    c = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, N)[:L]
    c = np.ascontiguousarray(c)
    np.maximum(c, 0.0, out=c)
    return c


def score_fast(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return float("inf")
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(a)):
        return float("inf")
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if s < 0.01:
        return float("inf")
    c = conv_c(a)
    if c.size == 0:
        return float("inf")
    m = float(np.max(c))
    return float(2.0 * n * m / (s * s))


def c_p_d(a, lam, s_target=None):
    """
    Compute c = a*a, softmax weights p on c, and the true gradient d of
    E_p[c] with respect to a:
        d_i = 2 * sum_k p_k a_{k-i}  over valid k-i.
    """
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.empty(0), np.empty(0), np.empty(0), 0.0
    if s_target is None:
        s_target = float(np.sum(a))
    s_target = float(max(1e-9, s_target))

    L = 2 * n - 1
    N = _next_pow2(3 * n - 2 if n > 1 else 1)

    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    c = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, N)[:L]
    c = np.ascontiguousarray(c)
    np.maximum(c, 0.0, out=c)

    peak_scale = (s_target * s_target) / float(n)
    if peak_scale <= 1e-12:
        peak_scale = 1.0
    beta = float(lam) / peak_scale
    if not np.isfinite(beta) or beta <= 0.0:
        return c, np.ones(L) / float(L), np.zeros(n), float(np.max(c))

    m = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(m):
        return c, np.ones(L) / float(L), np.zeros(n), 1e100
    arg = beta * (c - m)
    ex = np.exp(arg)
    W = float(np.sum(ex))
    if W <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(W):
        p = np.ones(L) / float(L)
    else:
        p = ex / W

    a_rev = a[::-1]
    fp = np.fft.rfft(p, N)
    fr = np.fft.rfft(a_rev, N)
    cross_full = np.fft.irfft(fp * fr, N)
    cross = cross_full[n - 1:2 * n - 1]
    cross = np.ascontiguousarray(cross)
    np.maximum(cross, 0.0, out=cross)

    d = 2.0 * cross
    np.maximum(d, 0.0, out=d)

    loss = m + float(np.log(max(W, 1e-300))) / beta
    if not np.isfinite(loss):
        loss = 1e100
    return c, p, d, float(loss)


def expand_theta(theta, n, ref):
    n = int(max(1, n))
    w = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    ref = int(ref)
    if n == 1:
        w[0] = 0.0
        return w
    ref = min(max(0, ref), n - 1)
    theta = np.asarray(theta, dtype=np.float64)
    if theta.size != n - 1:
        theta = np.resize(theta, n - 1)
    if ref == 0:
        w[0] = 0.0
        w[1:] = theta
    elif ref == n - 1:
        w[:-1] = theta
        w[-1] = 0.0
    else:
        w[:ref] = theta[:ref]
        w[ref] = 0.0
        w[ref + 1:] = theta[ref:]
    return w


def remove_ref(v, n, ref):
    n = int(max(1, n))
    if n == 1:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)
    ref = int(ref)
    ref = min(max(0, ref), n - 1)
    v = np.asarray(v, dtype=np.float64)
    if ref == 0:
        return v[1:]
    if ref == n - 1:
        return v[:-1]
    out = np.empty(n - 1, dtype=np.float64)
    out[:ref] = v[:ref]
    out[ref:] = v[ref + 1:]
    return out


def make_theta(a, ref=None):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    if n <= 1:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)
    if ref is None:
        ref = int(np.argmax(a))
    ref = min(max(0, int(ref)), n - 1)
    a = normalize_sum(a)
    if a[ref] <= 1e-300:
        a = a.copy()
        a[ref] = 1e-6
        a = normalize_sum(a)
    base = a / a[ref]
    base = np.maximum(base, 1e-100)
    w = np.log(base)
    w[ref] = 0.0
    theta = remove_ref(w, n, ref)
    theta = np.where(np.isfinite(theta), theta, -100.0)
    return np.clip(theta, -100.0, 100.0)


def theta_to_a(theta, n, ref, s_target):
    n = int(max(1, n))
    s_target = float(max(1e-9, s_target))
    w = expand_theta(theta, n, ref)
    w = np.where(np.isfinite(w), w, -100.0)
    wmax = float(np.max(w)) if n > 0 else 0.0
    e = np.exp(w - wmax)
    s = float(np.sum(e))
    if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        a = np.full(n, s_target / float(n), dtype=np.float64)
    else:
        a = s_target * e / s
    return np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)


def loss_from_theta(theta, n, lam, ref, s_target):
    n = int(n)
    if n == 0:
        return 1e100
    a = theta_to_a(theta, n, ref, s_target)
    c = conv_c(a)
    if c.size == 0 or not np.all(np.isfinite(c)):
        return 1e100
    m = float(np.max(c))
    peak_scale = (float(s_target) * float(s_target)) / float(n)
    if peak_scale <= 1e-12:
        peak_scale = 1.0
    beta = float(lam) / peak_scale
    if beta <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(beta):
        return 1e100
    arg = beta * (c - m)
    W = float(np.sum(np.exp(arg)))
    if W <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(W):
        return 1e100
    loss = m + float(np.log(max(W, 1e-300))) / beta
    if not np.isfinite(loss):
        return 1e100
    return float(loss)


def grad_from_theta(theta, n, lam, ref, s_target):
    n = int(n)
    if n <= 1:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)
    a = theta_to_a(theta, n, ref, s_target)
    c, p, d, loss = c_p_d(a, lam, s_target)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(d)):
        return np.zeros(n - 1, dtype=np.float64)
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if s <= 1e-14:
        return np.zeros(n - 1, dtype=np.float64)
    mu = float(np.dot(a, d)) / s
    gw = a * (d - mu)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(gw)):
        gw = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    g = remove_ref(gw, n, ref)
    g = np.where(np.isfinite(g), g, 0.0)
    return np.clip(g, -1e6, 1e6)


def minimize_callback(x):
    st = MIN_STATE
    if not st:
        return False
    now = time.monotonic()
    if now >= float(st.get("deadline", 0.0)):
        try:
            a = theta_to_a(x, int(st["n"]), int(st["ref"]), float(st["S"]))
            sc = score_fast(a)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < float(st["best_score"]):
                st["best_score"] = sc
                st["best_a"] = a.copy()
        except Exception:
            pass
        return True

    st["calls"] = int(st.get("calls", 0)) + 1
    do_update = (st["calls"] % 5 == 0) or (now >= float(st.get("next_update", 0.0)))
    if do_update:
        try:
            a = theta_to_a(x, int(st["n"]), int(st["ref"]), float(st["S"]))
            sc = score_fast(a)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < float(st["best_score"]):
                st["best_score"] = sc
                st["best_a"] = a.copy()
            st["next_update"] = now + 0.75
        except Exception:
            pass
    return False


def simple_smooth_phase(theta, n, ref, s_target, lam, budget, a_init, score_init):
    """Minimal fallback smooth line-search if SciPy is unavailable."""
    n = int(n)
    if n <= 1 or budget <= 0.0:
        return a_init, score_init
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    deadline = t0 + float(budget)
    y = np.asarray(theta, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    best_a = np.asarray(a_init, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    best_score = float(score_init)
    lr = 0.25
    stall = 0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline - 0.2:
        a = theta_to_a(y, n, ref, s_target)
        c, p, d, loss = c_p_d(a, lam, s_target)
        if not np.isfinite(loss):
            break
        score = 2.0 * n * float(np.max(c)) / (float(s_target) * float(s_target))
        if np.isfinite(score) and score < best_score:
            best_score = score
            best_a = a.copy()

        s = float(np.sum(a))
        if s <= 0.0:
            break
        mu = float(np.dot(a, d)) / s
        gw = a * (d - mu)
        g = remove_ref(gw, n, ref)
        if not np.all(np.isfinite(g)) or float(np.max(np.abs(g))) < 1e-30:
            break

        step = lr
        improved = False
        for _ in range(6):
            y_trial = y - step * g
            a_trial = theta_to_a(y_trial, n, ref, s_target)
            _, _, _, loss_trial = c_p_d(a_trial, lam, s_target)
            if np.isfinite(loss_trial) and loss_trial < loss * (1.0 + 1e-12):
                y = y_trial
                improved = True
                break
            step *= 0.5

        if improved:
            stall = 0
            lr = min(1.0, lr * 1.05)
        else:
            stall += 1
            lr = max(0.03, lr * 0.7)
            if stall > 80:
                break
    return best_a, best_score


def run_lbf_phase(theta, n, ref, s_target, lam, budget, a_now, score_now):
    n = int(n)
    if n <= 1 or budget <= 5.0:
        return a_now, score_now, theta

    theta = np.asarray(theta, dtype=np.float64)
    if theta.size != n - 1:
        theta = np.resize(theta, n - 1)

    if not HAS_SCIPY:
        a_best, sc_best = simple_smooth_phase(theta, n, ref, s_target, lam, budget, a_now, score_now)
        return a_best, sc_best, theta

    MIN_STATE.clear()
    MIN_STATE["deadline"] = time.monotonic() + float(budget)
    MIN_STATE["n"] = n
    MIN_STATE["ref"] = ref
    MIN_STATE["S"] = float(s_target)
    MIN_STATE["best_score"] = float(score_now)
    MIN_STATE["best_a"] = np.asarray(a_now, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    MIN_STATE["calls"] = 0
    MIN_STATE["next_update"] = time.monotonic()

    bounds = [(-BOUND, BOUND)] * (n - 1)
    options = {
        "maxiter": 5000,
        "maxfun": 200000,
        "maxls": 25,
        "ftol": 1e-12,
        "gtol": 1e-10,
    }

    res = None
    try:
        res = optimize.minimize(
            loss_from_theta,
            theta,
            jac=grad_from_theta,
            method="L-BFGS-B",
            args=(n, float(lam), int(ref), float(s_target)),
            bounds=bounds,
            callback=minimize_callback,
            options=options,
        )
    except Exception:
        res = None

    best_a = np.asarray(MIN_STATE.get("best_a", a_now), dtype=np.float64).copy()
    best_score = float(MIN_STATE.get("best_score", score_now))
    final_theta = theta

    if res is not None and hasattr(res, "x"):
        final_theta = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64)
        if final_theta.size == n - 1:
            try:
                a_f = theta_to_a(final_theta, n, ref, s_target)
                sc_f = score_fast(a_f)
                if np.isfinite(sc_f) and sc_f < best_score:
                    best_score = sc_f
                    best_a = a_f
            except Exception:
                pass

    return best_a, best_score, final_theta


def annealed_lbf(a, budget, seed=0):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    if n <= 1 or budget <= 0.0:
        return a, score_fast(a)

    s_target = target_sum_for_length(n)
    a = normalize_sum(a, s_target)
    best_a = a.copy()
    best_score = score_fast(a)
    if not np.isfinite(best_score):
        best_a = np.full(n, s_target / float(n))
        best_score = 2.0
        a = best_a

    if budget < 20.0:
        return best_a, best_score

    t0 = time.monotonic()
    deadline = t0 + float(budget)
    phases = [
        (12.0, 0.18),
        (24.0, 0.26),
        (48.0, 0.28),
        (96.0, 0.18),
        (192.0, 0.10),
    ]
    time_total = max(1.0, float(budget) - 5.0)

    for lam, frac in phases:
        if time.monotonic() >= deadline - 2.0:
            break
        tt = time_total * frac
        if tt < 12.0:
            continue
        ref = int(np.argmax(a))
        theta = make_theta(a, ref)
        a_now, sc_now, _ = run_lbf_phase(theta, n, ref, s_target, lam, tt, a, best_score)
        if a_now is not None and np.isfinite(sc_now) and sc_now < best_score:
            best_score = sc_now
            best_a = a_now
        # Continue from the best exact candidate found so far.
        a = best_a.copy()

    return best_a, best_score


def gauss_newton_polish(a, budget, seed=0):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    if n <= 2 or budget <= 0.0:
        return a, score_fast(a)

    s_target = target_sum_for_length(n)
    a = normalize_sum(a, s_target)
    best_a = a.copy()
    best_score = score_fast(a)
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    deadline = t0 + float(budget)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    L = 2 * n - 1
    K = min(L, max(48, n // 8))
    K = min(K, 256)
    no_improve = 0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline - 1.0:
        c = conv_c(a)
        if c.size == 0 or not np.all(np.isfinite(c)):
            break
        maxc = float(np.max(c))
        s = float(np.sum(a))
        if s <= 0.0:
            break
        score = float(2.0 * n * maxc / (s * s))
        if np.isfinite(score) and score < best_score:
            best_score = score
            best_a = a.copy()

        if K > L:
            K = L
        if K <= 0:
            break

        if K < L:
            idx = np.argpartition(c, -K)[-K:]
        else:
            idx = np.arange(L)
        ck = c[idx]

        D = np.empty((K, n), dtype=np.float64)
        for row, k in enumerate(idx):
            k = int(k)
            start = max(0, k - (n - 1))
            stop = min(n, k + 1)
            if stop <= start:
                continue
            ii = np.arange(start, stop)
            D[row, start:stop] = 2.0 * a[k - ii]

        G = D @ D.T
        if not np.all(np.isfinite(G)):
            break
        mean_diag = float(np.trace(G)) / float(K) if K > 0 else 1.0
        if not np.isfinite(mean_diag) or mean_diag <= 0.0:
            mean_diag = 1.0
        mu = 1e-6 * mean_diag + 1e-12
        I = np.eye(K, dtype=np.float64)

        maxk = float(np.max(ck))
        meank = float(np.mean(ck))
        targets = [meank, 0.999 * maxk, 0.995 * maxk]

        best_cand = None
        best_trial = score
        best_delta = None
        best_alpha = 0.0

        for T in targets:
            T = float(T)
            if not np.isfinite(T):
                continue
            y = T - ck
            if float(np.max(np.abs(y))) <= 1e-9 * max(1.0, maxk):
                continue
            try:
                gamma = np.linalg.solve(G + mu * I, y)
            except Exception:
                try:
                    gamma = np.linalg.lstsq(G, y, rcond=None)[0]
                except Exception:
                    continue
            if not np.all(np.isfinite(gamma)):
                continue
            delta = D.T @ gamma
            if not np.all(np.isfinite(delta)):
                continue
            delta = np.asarray(delta, dtype=np.float64)
            delta -= float(np.mean(delta))
            if float(np.max(np.abs(delta))) <= 1e-14:
                continue

            limit = 1.0
            neg = delta < 0.0
            if np.any(neg):
                lim2 = float(np.min(a[neg] / (-delta[neg]))) * 0.99
                if lim2 > 0.0:
                    limit = min(limit, lim2)
            if limit <= 0.0:
                continue

            alphas = [1.0, 0.5, 0.25, 0.125, 0.0625]
            if limit >= 0.999:
                alphas = [2.0, 1.0, 0.5, 0.25, 0.125]
            for alpha in alphas:
                aa = alpha * limit
                if aa <= 1e-12:
                    continue
                cand = a + aa * delta
                if cand.size and float(np.min(cand)) < -1e-9:
                    continue
                cand = np.clip(cand, 0.0, None)
                cand = normalize_sum(cand, s_target)
                sc = score_fast(cand)
                if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_trial:
                    best_trial = sc
                    best_cand = cand
                    best_delta = delta
                    best_alpha = aa

        if best_cand is not None and best_trial < score * (1.0 - 1e-12):
            a = best_cand
            if best_trial < best_score:
                best_score = best_trial
                best_a = a.copy()
            no_improve = 0
        else:
            no_improve += 1
            if no_improve == 3:
                a = smooth_perturb(a, rng, 0.12)
                no_improve = 0
            K = max(24, int(0.75 * K))
            if no_improve > 7 or K < 24:
                break

    return best_a, best_score


def add_candidate_sequence(pool, seq):
    if seq is None:
        return
    try:
        seq = normalize_sum(seq)
        sc = score_fast(seq)
        if np.isfinite(sc):
            pool.append((sc, seq))
    except Exception:
        return


def generate_starts(n, best_seq, parent_seq, seed, random_count=30):
    n = int(max(1, n))
    s_target = target_sum_for_length(n)
    pool = []
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    if best_seq is not None:
        add_candidate_sequence(pool, upsample_sequence(best_seq, n))
    if parent_seq is not None:
        add_candidate_sequence(pool, upsample_sequence(parent_seq, n))
    add_candidate_sequence(pool, np.ones(n))

    t = (np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) + 0.5) / float(n)

    for alpha in (-0.8, -0.5, -0.3, -0.15, 0.15, 0.3, 0.5, 0.8):
        add_candidate_sequence(pool, 1.0 + alpha * (2.0 * t - 1.0))
        add_candidate_sequence(pool, 1.0 + alpha * np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * t))
        add_candidate_sequence(pool, np.exp(0.6 * alpha * (2.0 * t - 1.0)))
        add_candidate_sequence(pool, np.exp(0.6 * alpha * np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * t)))

    # Piecewise constant starts.
    for _ in range(10):
        nb = int(rng.integers(4, 18))
        nb = min(max(2, nb), max(2, n // 2))
        vals = rng.uniform(0.1, 3.0, nb)
        if rng.random() < 0.25:
            vals[int(rng.integers(0, nb))] = 0.0
        edges = np.round(np.linspace(0.0, n, nb + 1)).astype(int)
        edges[0] = 0
        edges[-1] = n
        lengths = np.diff(edges)
        good = lengths > 0
        if np.any(good):
            arr = np.repeat(vals[good], lengths[good])
            add_candidate_sequence(pool, arr)

    # Smooth random Fourier starts.
    random_count = int(max(0, random_count))
    for _ in range(random_count):
        z = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
        for m in range(1, 7):
            amp = rng.normal(0.0, 1.0 / math.sqrt(float(m)))
            ph = rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi)
            z += amp * np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * m * t + ph)
        add_candidate_sequence(pool, np.exp(0.7 * z))

    if not pool:
        pool.append((2.0, normalize_sum(np.ones(n), s_target)))
    pool.sort(key=_pair_score)
    return pool[:5]


def smooth_perturb(a, rng, strength=0.3):
    if a is None:
        return normalize_sum(np.ones(512))
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    s_target = target_sum_for_length(n)
    a = normalize_sum(a, s_target)
    if n <= 2:
        return a

    t = (np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) + 0.5) / float(n)
    z = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    for m in range(1, 5):
        amp = float(strength) / float(m + 1)
        ph = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi))
        z += amp * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * m * t + ph)
    a *= np.exp(z)

    if rng.random() < 0.45:
        width = int(max(1, n // 50))
        start = int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n - width + 1)))
        stop = min(n, start + width)
        a[start:stop] *= np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, float(strength), stop - start))

    return normalize_sum(a, s_target)


def run_length(n, budget, global_best, global_score, seed, parent_seq):
    n = int(max(1, n))
    budget = float(max(0.0, budget))
    if budget < 20.0:
        if global_best is None:
            seq = normalize_sum(np.ones(n))
        else:
            seq = upsample_sequence(global_best, n)
        return seq, score_fast(seq)

    deadline = time.monotonic() + budget
    random_count = 16 if n > 2048 else 30
    starts = generate_starts(n, global_best, parent_seq, seed, random_count)
    if not starts:
        best_a = normalize_sum(np.ones(n))
        best_score = score_fast(best_a)
        return best_a, best_score

    best_a, best_score = starts[0]

    # Main smooth optimization over the best starts.
    smooth_budget = 0.58 * budget
    gn_budget = 0.28 * budget
    restart_budget = max(0.0, budget - smooth_budget - gn_budget)

    top = starts[:3]
    frac1 = [0.55, 0.30, 0.15]
    for i, item in enumerate(top):
        if i >= len(frac1):
            break
        if time.monotonic() >= deadline - 30.0:
            break
        tt = smooth_budget * frac1[i]
        if tt < 15.0:
            continue
        sc0, a0 = item
        a_new, sc_new = annealed_lbf(a0, tt, int(seed) + 100 * i)
        if a_new is not None and np.isfinite(sc_new):
            if sc_new < best_score:
                best_score = sc_new
                best_a = a_new
            if sc_new < global_score:
                global_score = sc_new
                global_best = a_new

    # Gauss-Newton polishing.
    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 15.0 and gn_budget > 15.0:
        a_gn, sc_gn = gauss_newton_polish(best_a, gn_budget, int(seed) + 777)
        if a_gn is not None and np.isfinite(sc_gn):
            if sc_gn < best_score:
                best_score = sc_gn
                best_a = a_gn
            if sc_gn < global_score:
                global_score = sc_gn
                global_best = a_gn

    # Cheap random smooth restarts.
    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 25.0 and restart_budget > 30.0:
        rng = np.random.default_rng(int(seed) + 4242)
        remaining = max(0.0, deadline - time.monotonic() - 20.0)
        num = int(max(1, remaining / 75.0))
        num = min(4, num)
        each = max(20.0, remaining / float(num))
        for r in range(num):
            if time.monotonic() >= deadline - 25.0:
                break
            a_pert = smooth_perturb(global_best, rng, 0.35)
            ts = 0.62 * each
            if ts > 15.0:
                a_r, sc_r = annealed_lbf(a_pert, ts, int(seed) + 9000 + 10 * r)
                if a_r is not None and np.isfinite(sc_r) and sc_r < global_score:
                    global_score = sc_r
                    global_best = a_r
            tg = 0.32 * each
            if tg > 15.0:
                a_r, sc_r = gauss_newton_polish(global_best, tg, int(seed) + 12000 + 10 * r)
                if a_r is not None and np.isfinite(sc_r) and sc_r < global_score:
                    global_score = sc_r
                    global_best = a_r

    return global_best, global_score


def sanitize_for_return(seq):
    a = np.asarray(seq, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size == 0:
        return [1.0]
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if s < 0.01:
        return [1.0] * 512
    target = target_sum_for_length(a.size)
    scale = target / s
    if a.size > 0 and float(np.max(a)) * scale <= 1000.0:
        a = a * scale
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    if float(np.sum(a)) < 0.01:
        return [1.0] * 512
    return list(map(float, a))


def exact_score_list(lst):
    try:
        return float(evaluate_sequence(lst))
    except Exception:
        try:
            return score_fast(np.asarray(lst, dtype=np.float64))
        except Exception:
            return float("inf")


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    """
    Main entry point. Runs a multi-length annealed L-BFGS-B + Gauss-Newton
    search and returns the best valid sequence found.
    """
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    budget = float(max(0.0, budget_s))
    deadline = t0 + budget

    parent_arr = None
    try:
        parent_val = height_sequence_1
        if parent_val is not None:
            parent_arr = np.asarray(parent_val, dtype=np.float64)
            if parent_arr.size > 0 and np.any(np.isfinite(parent_arr)):
                parent_arr = normalize_sum(parent_arr)
    except Exception:
        parent_arr = None

    global_best = None
    global_score = float("inf")

    if parent_arr is not None and parent_arr.size > 0:
        sc = score_fast(parent_arr)
        if np.isfinite(sc):
            global_best = parent_arr
            global_score = sc

    if global_best is None or not np.isfinite(global_score):
        fallback = normalize_sum(np.ones(512))
        global_best = fallback
        global_score = score_fast(fallback)

    print("initial fast score = %.9f" % global_score)

    if budget < 25.0:
        return sanitize_for_return(global_best)

    if budget < 350.0:
        lengths = [512]
        weights = [1.0]
    elif budget < 1000.0:
        lengths = [512, 1024]
        weights = [1.0, 1.1]
    else:
        lengths = [512, 1024, 2048, 4096]
        weights = [1.0, 1.1, 1.4, 1.1]

    reserve = 90.0 if budget > 300.0 else 35.0
    avail = max(60.0, budget - reserve)
    total_w = float(sum(weights[:len(lengths)]))
    base = avail / total_w if total_w > 0 else avail

    for i, n in enumerate(lengths):
        if time.monotonic() >= deadline - reserve:
            break
        b = base * float(weights[i])
        b = min(b, max(0.0, deadline - time.monotonic() - reserve))
        if b < 35.0:
            break
        print("length %d, budget %.1f, current %.9f" % (n, b, global_score))
        try:
            a_new, sc_new = run_length(int(n), b, global_best, global_score, int(seed) + 1000 * i, parent_arr)
        except Exception:
            a_new, sc_new = global_best, global_score
        if a_new is not None and np.isfinite(sc_new) and sc_new < global_score:
            global_score = sc_new
            global_best = a_new
            print("improved at length %d: %.9f" % (n, global_score))

    # Final polish of the best sequence found so far.
    remain = max(0.0, deadline - time.monotonic() - reserve)
    if remain > 60.0 and global_best is not None:
        try:
            a_pol, sc_pol = gauss_newton_polish(global_best, remain, int(seed) + 5555)
            if a_pol is not None and np.isfinite(sc_pol) and sc_pol < global_score:
                global_score = sc_pol
                global_best = a_pol
                print("final polish improved: %.9f" % global_score)
        except Exception:
            pass

    # Exact validation of a few top candidates.
    exact_best_seq = np.asarray(global_best, dtype=np.float64)
    exact_best_score = float(global_score) if np.isfinite(global_score) else float("inf")

    validation_seq = [global_best, parent_arr, np.ones(512, dtype=np.float64)]
    for arr in validation_seq:
        if arr is None:
            continue
        if time.monotonic() >= deadline - 10.0:
            break
        try:
            lst = sanitize_for_return(arr)
            ex = exact_score_list(lst)
            if np.isfinite(ex) and ex < exact_best_score:
                exact_best_score = ex
                exact_best_seq = np.asarray(lst, dtype=np.float64)
        except Exception:
            pass

    print("best exact/scipy score returned = %.9f" % exact_best_score)
    return sanitize_for_return(exact_best_seq)
```
